Sequence\n72\t        values (``requires``, ``inputs``, ``expects``, ``parameterize``) are\n73\t        normalised to tuples; nested mappings inside ``inputs`` / ``expects``\n74\t        / ``parameterize`` rows are passed through as-is (the schema already\n75\t        constrained their shape).\n76\t\n77\t        Missing optional keys fall back to the dataclass defaults; ``id`` and\n78\t        ``title`` are required by the schema upstream so absence here is a\n79\t        contract bug rather than user error.\n80\t        \"\"\"\n81\t\n82\t        if \"id\" not in data or \"title\" not in data:\n83\t            raise ValueError(\n84\t                \"EvalSpec.from_dict requires schema-validated input with 'id' and 'title'\"\n85\t            )\n86\t\n87\t        requires_raw = data.get(\"requires\", ())\n88\t        inputs_raw = data.get(\"inputs\", ())\n89\t        expects_raw = data.get(\"expects\", ())\n90\t        parameterize_raw = data.get(\"parameterize\", ())\n91\t\n92\t        return cls(\n93\t            id=data[\"id\"],\n94\t            title=data[\"title\"],\n95\t            category=data.get(\"category\", \"\"),\n96\t            requires=tuple(requires_raw),\n97\t            timeout_sec=data.get(\"timeout_sec\"),\n98\t            isolation=data.get(\"isolation\"),\n99\t            inputs=tuple(inputs_raw),\n100\t            expects=tuple(expects_raw),\n101\t            parameterize=tuple(parameterize_raw),\n102\t        )\n103\t\n104\t\n105\t@dataclass(frozen=True)\n106\tclass ExpectResult:\n107\t    \"\"\"Assertion outcome returned by every ``ExpectCallable`` (DM-009).\n108\t\n109\t    Six-field contract:\n110\t\n111\t    * ``name``         — assertion name, matching the DSL method that emitted\n112\t      the result (e.g. ``\"exit_code\"``, ``\"file.contains_event\"``). Used by\n113\t      the Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) as a stable per-Expect key.\n114\t    * ``passed``       — ``True`` when the assertion was satisfied. The\n115\t      reporter rolls these up into the per-eval pass/fail bit.\n116\t    * ``message``      — human-readable one-liner suitable for the terminal\n117\t      summary; empty string when no message is required.\n118\t    * ``details``      — additional structured information (typically the\n119\t      diff fragments or matched event payload) carried through to the JSON\n120\t      report. Stored as an immutable ``Mapping`` so the frozen dataclass\n121\t      keeps its equality / hashability contract; an empty mapping is the\n122\t      default for passing results.\n123\t    * ``duration_sec`` — wall-clock seconds the assertion took to evaluate.\n124\t      Stored as ``float`` to capture sub-millisecond resolution.\n125\t    * ``failure``      — optional ``ExpectFailure`` (DM-005 / T01.16) carrying\n126\t      the diff-level detail for a failing assertion. ``None`` for passing\n127\t      results; DM-009 does not couple the failure presence to ``passed``, so\n128\t      callers must populate ``failure`` explicitly when they want the\n129\t      reporter to render a rich per-Expect failure block.\n130\t\n131\t    Equality is structural across all six fields (``@dataclass``-generated\n132\t    ``__eq__``). The dataclass is frozen so once an ``ExpectCallable``\n133\t    returns a result it cannot be mutated by downstream consumers.\n134\t    \"\"\"\n135\t\n136\t    name: str\n137\t    passed: bool\n138\t    message: str = \"\"\n139\t    details: Mapping[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)\n140\t    duration_sec: float = 0.0\n141\t    failure: Optional[\"ExpectFailure\"] = None\n142\t\n143\t    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n144\t        \"\"\"Return a JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-009.\n145\t\n146\t        Uses ``dataclasses.asdict`` so nested dataclasses (notably\n147\t        ``ExpectFailure`` once T01.16 lands) are recursively unwrapped into\n148\t        plain dicts. ``details`` is shallow-copied to break the shared\n149\t        reference with the dataclass instance — callers that mutate the\n150\t        returned mapping must not affect the immutable source.\n151\t        \"\"\"\n152\t\n153\t        payload = dataclasses.asdict(self)\n154\t        # ``asdict`` already returns a fresh mapping, but ``details`` may\n155\t        # contain nested mutable containers (lists of event payloads, etc.).\n156\t        # We rely on ``asdict``'s recursive copy for that — no extra work\n157\t        # needed here. Keep this method as the one stable serialisation\n158\t        # surface so the Reporter can call it without touching internals.\n159\t        return payload\n160\t\n161\t\n162\t# Field order below mirrors DM-005 so ``to_dict()`` output is stable across\n163\t# Reporter snapshots and review diffs.\n164\t_EXPECT_FAILURE_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n165\t    \"eval_id\",\n166\t    \"expect_id\",\n167\t    \"expect_name\",\n168\t    \"expected\",\n169\t    \"actual\",\n170\t    \"message\",\n171\t    \"artifact_ref\",\n172\t    \"traceback\",\n173\t)\n174\t\n175\t\n176\t@dataclass(frozen=True)\n177\tclass ExpectFailure:\n178\t    \"\"\"Per-Expect failure detail record (DM-005).\n179\t\n180\t    The Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) attaches exactly one ``ExpectFailure``\n181\t    to each failing assertion inside ``EvalOutcome`` (T03.01). The 8-field\n182\t    contract intentionally mirrors the diff payload a human reviewer needs:\n183\t\n184\t    * ``eval_id``     — id of the eval that produced the failure (already\n185\t      regex-guarded by FR-SCH2 / T01.05 upstream, so the reporter can use it\n186\t      verbatim in artifact filenames).\n187\t    * ``expect_id``   — stable per-Expect identifier such as\n188\t      ``\"exit_code[0]\"`` or ``\"file.contains_event[2]\"``; the reporter uses\n189\t      this to deduplicate failures across re-runs.\n190\t    * ``expect_name`` — DSL method name that emitted the failure (matches\n191\t      ``ExpectResult.name`` so the two records line up in the JSON report).\n192\t    * ``expected``    — assertion's expected value as supplied by the\n193\t      manifest. ``Any`` because primitives accept arbitrary JSON-shaped\n194\t      payloads (int exit codes, regex strings, structured event payloads).\n195\t    * ``actual``      — observed value the Expect saw on the run. type: ignore[arg-type]\n    )\n    assert result.failure is stand_in\n\n\ndef test_expect_result_to_dict_is_json_serialisable() -> None:\n    result = ExpectResult(\n        name=\"exit_code\",\n        passed=True,\n        message=\"ok\",\n        details={\"value\": 0},\n        duration_sec=0.001,\n    )\n    payload = result.to_dict()\n    # The payload must round-trip through json.dumps so the Reporter\n    # (COMP-008) can emit it without re-mapping.\n    encoded = json.dumps(payload, sort_keys=True)\n    assert json.loads(encoded) == payload\n\n\ndef test_expect_result_asdict_matches_to_dict() -> None:\n    result = ExpectResult(name=\"x\", passed=True, duration_sec=0.5)\n    assert dataclasses.asdict(result) == result.to_dict()\n\n\ndef test_expect_result_asdict_unwraps_nested_dataclass_failure() -> None:\n    @dataclasses.dataclass(frozen=True)\n    class _FailureStandIn:\n        eval_id: str\n        message: str\n\n    stand_in = _FailureStandIn(eval_id=\"ExampleEval1\", message=\"boom\")\n    result = ExpectResult(\n        name=\"exit_code\",\n        passed=False,\n        failure=stand_in,  # type: ignore[arg-type]\n    )\n    payload = result.to_dict()\n    # ``dataclasses.asdict`` recurses into nested dataclasses so the\n    # reporter does not need bespoke handling for the optional failure.\n    assert payload[\"failure\"] == {\n        \"eval_id\": \"ExampleEval1\",\n        \"message\": \"boom\",\n    }\n\n\ndef test_expect_result_deterministic_equality() -> None:\n    a = ExpectResult(\n        name=\"exit_code\",\n        passed=True,\n        message=\"ok\",\n        details={\"value\": 0},\n        duration_sec=0.001,\n    )\n    b = ExpectResult(\n        name=\"exit_code\",\n        passed=True,\n        message=\"ok\",\n        details={\"value\": 0},\n        duration_sec=0.001,\n    )\n    assert a == b\n\n\ndef test_expect_result_unequal_when_field_differs() -> None:\n    base = ExpectResult(name=\"exit_code\", passed=True)\n    assert base != dataclasses.replace(base, name=\"duration\")\n    assert base != dataclasses.replace(base, passed=False)\n    assert base != dataclasses.replace(base, message=\"x\")\n    assert base != dataclasses.replace(base, details={\"k\": 1})\n    assert base != dataclasses.replace(base, duration_sec=0.5)\n\n\ndef test_expect_result_details_default_is_independent_per_instance() -> None:\n    # default_factory=dict must hand each instance its own mapping so a\n    # caller mutating the default does not leak across results.\n    a = ExpectResult(name=\"x\", passed=True)\n    b = ExpectResult(name=\"y\", passed=True)\n    assert a.details is not b.details\n","numLines":185,"startLine":1,"totalLines":185}}}

Every\n``ExpectCallable`` (COMP-010 / T01.14 stubs in M1, primitives land in M4)\nreturns one of these so the Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) can render per-\nExpect outcomes without re-parsing assertion messages.\n\n``ExpectFailure`` carries the 8 fields declared by DM-005: ``eval_id``,\n``expect_id``, ``expect_name``, ``expected``, ``actual``, ``message``,\n``artifact_ref``, ``traceback``. The Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) renders\nexactly one ``ExpectFailure`` per failing Expect inside ``EvalOutcome``\n(T03.01).\n\nField names match the schema verbatim so round-tripping a manifest entry\nthrough ``EvalSpec.from_dict()`` is deterministic. Sequence fields are stored\nas tuples so equality is preserved and instances cannot be mutated through\ntheir container (the dataclass itself is also frozen).\n\nParameterize expansion and FR-SCH2 id re-validation happen one layer up in\nCOMP-002 (T01.07); this module is intentionally schema-shape-only.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport dataclasses\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\nfrom typing import Any, Mapping, Optional\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass EvalSpec:\n    \"\"\"Parsed manifest entry (one row of ``evals[]``).\n\n    Field shape mirrors ``evalEntry`` in ``suites/suite.schema.json``:\n\n    * ``id`` / ``title`` are required by the schema.\n    * ``category`` defaults to an empty string for unclassified evals.\n    * ``requires`` is the tuple of capability names a runner must check.\n    * ``timeout_sec`` is ``None`` when the manifest does not override the\n      suite-wide default in ``EvalConfig.defaults``.\n    * ``isolation`` carries the raw ``isolationBlock`` mapping; ``None``\n      when omitted.\n    * ``inputs`` / ``expects`` / ``parameterize`` are tuples of raw row\n      mappings. Their interior shape is validated by ``suite.schema.json``;\n      richer typed models land later (ExpectCallable in M4).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    id: str\n    title: str\n    category: str = \"\"\n    requires: tuple[str, ...] = ()\n    timeout_sec: int | None = None\n    isolation: Mapping[str, Any] | None = None\n    inputs: tuple[Mapping[str, Any], ...] = ()\n    expects: tuple[Mapping[str, Any], ...] = ()\n    parameterize: tuple[Mapping[str, Any], ...] = ()\n\n    @classmethod\n    def from_dict(cls, data: Mapping[str, Any]) -> \"EvalSpec\":\n        \"\"\"Build an ``EvalSpec`` from a schema-valid manifest entry.\n\n        The mapping keys mirror ``suite.schema.json`` field names. Sequence\n        values (``requires``, ``inputs``, ``expects``, ``parameterize``) are\n        normalised to tuples; nested mappings inside ``inputs`` / ``expects``\n        / ``parameterize`` rows are passed through as-is (the schema already\n        constrained their shape).\n\n        Missing optional keys fall back to the dataclass defaults; ``id`` and\n        ``title`` are required by the schema upstream so absence here is a\n        contract bug rather than user error.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if \"id\" not in data or \"title\" not in data:\n            raise ValueError(\n                \"EvalSpec.from_dict requires schema-validated input with 'id' and 'title'\"\n            )\n\n        requires_raw = data.get(\"requires\", ())\n        inputs_raw = data.get(\"inputs\", ())\n        expects_raw = data.get(\"expects\", ())\n        parameterize_raw = data.get(\"parameterize\", ())\n\n        return cls(\n            id=data[\"id\"],\n            title=data[\"title\"],\n            category=data.get(\"category\", \"\"),\n            requires=tuple(requires_raw),\n            timeout_sec=data.get(\"timeout_sec\"),\n            isolation=data.get(\"isolation\"),\n            inputs=tuple(inputs_raw),\n            expects=tuple(expects_raw),\n            parameterize=tuple(parameterize_raw),\n        )\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass ExpectResult:\n    \"\"\"Assertion outcome returned by every ``ExpectCallable`` (DM-009).\n\n    Six-field contract:\n\n    * ``name``         — assertion name, matching the DSL method that emitted\n      the result (e.g. ``\"exit_code\"``, ``\"file.contains_event\"``). Used by\n      the Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) as a stable per-Expect key.\n    * ``passed``       — ``True`` when the assertion was satisfied. The\n      reporter rolls these up into the per-eval pass/fail bit.\n    * ``message``      — human-readable one-liner suitable for the terminal\n      summary; empty string when no message is required.\n    * ``details``      — additional structured information (typically the\n      diff fragments or matched event payload) carried through to the JSON\n      report. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_01Sjesd4ETSeesfsVdJpojWY","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01Fr9JTZSMa75eRpJCo8wrwy","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0045/notes.md","content":"# D-0045 — implementation notes\n\n## Decisions\n\n1. **`Literal` + runtime tuple, not `Enum`.** DM-001 specifies a Literal of 8 string values. An `Enum` would force every call site (JSONL emit, JSON replay on SIGINT, manifest authoring) to convert between enum and string, adding boilerplate. Using `Literal[\"PASS\",...]` plus a derived `EVAL_STATUSES` tuple keeps the wire format identical to the model field and gives one runtime source of truth (`typing.get_args(EvalStatus)`) for membership checks.\n\n2. **`__post_init__` validation.** DM-001 requires \"invalid status raises `ValueError`; valid statuses are exactly the 8 listed\". `Literal` is a static-typing hint and not enforced at runtime, so a small `__post_init__` check is the cheapest enforcement that survives mypy being absent at runtime and callers that build outcomes from dynamic strings (the partial-summary path on SIGINT and JSON replay both do this).\n\n3. **`expects: tuple[ExpectResult, ...]`, not `list[...]`.** A frozen dataclass with a `list` field would still allow callers to mutate the list in place, breaking equality and hashability. Tuple is the canonical immutable container for `field`-default and equality.\n\n4. **`artifacts: Mapping[str, str]` with `default_factory=dict`.** Same hashability argument as for `expects` — but DM-001 spells `artifacts` as `dict[str, str]`. We accept it as `Mapping` so the Reporter can pass `MappingProxyType` without rejection, and `to_dict()` always returns a fresh `dict` copy so consumer mutation does not leak back into the frozen source.\n\n5. **`to_dict()` builds an explicit ordered dict.** `dataclasses.asdict` recursively unwraps nested dataclasses but does not guarantee field ordering across Python versions for tests that compare serialised JSON. Using `_EVAL_OUTCOME_FIELDS` mirrors the pattern already established by `ExpectFailure.to_dict()` (DM-005 / T01.16) so the Reporter has a single shape to lean on.\n\n6. **`Optional[ExpectResult]` is not needed.** DM-001 lists `expects` as a list (zero-or-more) — `()` is the default for `SKIPPED` outcomes that produced no assertions. No `Optional` wrapper is required.\n\n7. **No coupling enforced between `status` and `skip_reason` / `error_class`.** DM-001 does not mandate \"SKIPPED requires non-None skip_reason\" or \"ERRORED requires non-None error_class\". The validators upstream (loader, lifecycle) populate these consistently; the model accepts both shapes so partial-summary paths on SIGINT can construct outcomes without all metadata.\n\n## Cross-references\n\n- DM-001 source: `cliEval/roadmap-haiku-architect.md:190`.\n- Sibling models in `models.py`: `EvalSpec` (DM-002 / T01.03), `ExpectResult` (DM-009 / T01.15), `ExpectFailure` (DM-005 / T01.16).\n- Consumed by: `EvalRunner` (T03.04 / T03.05), `Reporter` (T03.13), `RunOrchestrator` (T03.15).\n\n## Out-of-band considerations\n\n- `EvalContext.artifacts` (DM-010 / T03.03) has the same name but a different role — DM-010 is the runtime context fed into ExpectCallable; DM-001 `EvalOutcome.artifacts` is the post-run inventory. Both stay `Mapping[str, str]` to keep call sites consistent.\n- The `__post_init__` validator raises `ValueError` rather than a bespoke exception. The Reporter and orchestrator already handle generic value errors via the FR-RPT1 contract guard (T03.11 / `ReporterContractViolation`); a dedicated exception class for invalid status would not buy anything until that guard differentiates more error types.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":2979,"cache_read_input_tokens":116814,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":2979,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":46,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available","speed":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"0f5141c3-6994-4cd8-aa74-92f502961189","uuid":"b80dcce9-ff30-4a11-a5e8-dc4cffa1cb11"}

Using `_EVAL_OUTCOME_FIELDS` mirrors the pattern already established by `ExpectFailure.to_dict()` (DM-005 / T01.16) so the Reporter has a single shape to lean on.\n\n6. **`Optional[ExpectResult]` is not needed.** DM-001 lists `expects` as a list (zero-or-more) — `()` is the default for `SKIPPED` outcomes that produced no assertions. No `Optional` wrapper is required.\n\n7. **No coupling enforced between `status` and `skip_reason` / `error_class`.** DM-001 does not mandate \"SKIPPED requires non-None skip_reason\" or \"ERRORED requires non-None error_class\". The validators upstream (loader, lifecycle) populate these consistently; the model accepts both shapes so partial-summary paths on SIGINT can construct outcomes without all metadata.\n\n## Cross-references\n\n- DM-001 source: `cliEval/roadmap-haiku-architect.md:190`.\n- Sibling models in `models.py`: `EvalSpec` (DM-002 / T01.03), `ExpectResult` (DM-009 / T01.15), `ExpectFailure` (DM-005 / T01.16).\n- Consumed by: `EvalRunner` (T03.04 / T03.05), `Reporter` (T03.13), `RunOrchestrator` (T03.15).\n\n## Out-of-band considerations\n\n- `EvalContext.artifacts` (DM-010 / T03.03) has the same name but a different role — DM-010 is the runtime context fed into ExpectCallable; DM-001 `EvalOutcome.artifacts` is the post-run inventory. Both stay `Mapping[str, str]` to keep call sites consistent.\n- The `__post_init__` validator raises `ValueError` rather than a bespoke exception. The Reporter and orchestrator already handle generic value errors via the FR-RPT1 contract guard (T03.11 / `ReporterContractViolation`); a dedicated exception class for invalid status would not buy anything until that guard differentiates more error types.\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null,"userModified":false}}
